# Shared changes

Fenced files (`docs/WORKFLOW.md` section 4) are never edited by a screen or
feature task. A task that needs one changed adds a row here and builds
everything else. The owner applies the queue between rounds of work, then the
task that asked for it finishes.

Four queues, because they have different owners and triggers:

## 1. Requests (a prop, token, route, schema field or client method)

| KYC-02 #3 | KYC-02 | wawu-backend `src/common/filters/all-exceptions.filter.ts` | A body the global body parser refuses is answered `500 "Something went wrong"` on every route today (seen on the built app: a 150 KB selfie body). Change: when the exception is not an HttpException but carries a numeric `status` from 400 to 499 and `expose: true` (the `http-errors` the parser throws: `entity.too.large` 413, `entity.parse.failed` 400), answer that status with a fixed sentence (413 "That is too large to send.", 400 "That request could not be read."), never the parser's own message. Every other exception is formatted as now. The selfie route's own cap (100,000 base64 characters) sits below the 100 KB limit, so the app never meets it; this only makes the refusal honest. | made by FIX-08 on branch `wmt/FIX-08` (backend), for approval at merge. As asked, with three differences: 415 is mapped too (a charset or content encoding the parser cannot read); only 400, 413 and 415 are mapped, so any other 4xx that merely looks like `http-errors` stays a 500 as before; and malformed JSON keeps the 400 Nest already gave it, with the parser's own message (the FIX-08 brief: today's 400s stay as they are). (Android needs no extra text; `recordAudioAndroid: false`, no microphone), and a new development build. Why: the selfie is matched with the BVN photo and must be taken on the phone, downscaled and re-encoded so it fits the server's limits (100,000 base64 characters, 2,048 px a side) and carries nothing after the image's end (KYC-02 refuses such files). Until both are installed the phone build shows A6's "We can't open your camera" state (`src/features/open-wallet/selfie/capture.ts`), and the browser build takes the photo through the browser's picker (`capture.web.ts`). Once applied, KYC-03 fills `capture.ts` in: `CameraView` (front lens) as the oval's `Preview`, `takePictureAsync`, then `ImageManipulator` resize to 640 on the long side and JPEG at falling quality until it fits, returning plain base64. V10 (the phone check) waits on it. | applied (SHARED-04, 41e35ee): `expo-camera` with this row's camera wording and no microphone (iOS string removed, `recordAudioAndroid: false`, `RECORD_AUDIO` blocked), `expo-image-manipulator`. `capture.ts` fills the oval with the front camera once allowed (the first tap asks; a camera turned off offers the phone's settings), takes the camera's in-memory picture, resizes it to 640 on the long side and re-encodes JPEG at 0.85, 0.7, 0.55 then 0.4 until it fits 100,000 characters, never sends one without the JPEG end marker last, over 2,048 px or under the server's smallest, and deletes the manipulator's cache file (`expo-file-system`). V10 waits on the lead's preview build. Round 2 (6c1e286): the phone's check is the server's JPEG walk ported line for line (a JPEG with a second JPEG appended is refused, as the server refuses it); the encode deletes any file the manipulator wrote meanwhile, including one written before a failure; the verifier's boundary specs are in the suite (`selfieLimits.test.tsx`). The iOS camera string stays as is: EVENTS-09 updates it when it scans tickets |

A write that runs out of time throws `WawuIdTimeoutException`, a `BadGatewayException` (502) carrying the words that write already answers when WAWU ID fails (verification and name unchanged; the tier call, which answered a bare 500, now answers 502 "WAWU ID did not answer in time. Schema: two nullable columns, `AccountDeletion.purgeNote` and (round 6) `AccountDeletion.walletSyncedAt` (set on a cancelled row once a reconcile confirmed its wallet unfrozen, so the hourly pass skips it; null: a check is still owed), and a wider status check (`freezing` and `purging` join `scheduled`, `cancelled`, `purged`); both columns are in the same unmerged migration, no row is touched; `rollback.sql` drops both columns and refuses while a deletion is freezing or purging. Contract (amends #2): the `cancel-deletion` answer gains two optional fields, `deletionInProgress` and `message`, set only once the purge has started; nothing removed or retyped. `AccountPurgeService.purge` now also returns `failed` (the tables it could not clear) and `keptForBuyers`, and no longer deletes a content piece a buyer paid for (`Purchase.content` is Restrict): that piece is hidden and loses its title, description, preview, files and slug. Callers that read `deleted`, `total` and `pointsLeft` are unchanged. Behaviour on `DELETE /account` (a protected route) is as before for a member with no wallet. | open Round 7 (lead rulings 16 Oct 2026): a third additive migration, `20261024170000_account_deletion_pass_claim` (one nullable column, `AccountDeletion.reconcileClaimedUntil`, the hourly pass's claim on a row; `rollback.sql` drops it); the first two migrations are restamped `20261024150000_account_deletion` and `20261024160000_account_deletion_states` (their `rollback.sql` names follow). Owner ruling: `AccountPurgeService.purge` no longer strips a content piece with a completed paid purchase: it stays whole (files, lessons, frames), hidden (`removed`), with `creatorWawuId` set to `deleted-account` and its storage rows filed under that id; `GET /content/:id` and `GET /content/:id/detail` let a buyer with a completed purchase read such a piece (the only removed piece anyone can read), and the creator card of that id reads "Deleted account". Pieces with only an unfinished purchase are stripped as before; unsold pieces and their storage rows are deleted as before. No route, field or contract change (contract:build twice equal, no diff). Round 8 (lead rulings 17 Oct 2026): no schema, route or contract change (contract:build twice equal, no diff). The `rollback.sql` of `20261024160000_account_deletion_states` and of `20261024150000_account_deletion` now also refuse while a cancelled deletion still owes an unfreeze (`walletSyncedAt` null). Behaviour: a buyer with a completed paid purchase opens a piece a deletion hid on `GET /content/:id`, `/detail` and `/media` from the moment of `DELETE` (not only after the purge); everyone else, the creator included, still gets 404; a non-buyer also gets 404 on that piece's comments (read, post, like), save and `POST /content/:id/rate` (every other removed piece answers as before, so the web's routes are unchanged); `AccountPurgeService.purge` replaces email addresses and phone numbers in a kept piece's title and description with `[removed]`, re-keys the pieces it strips for a pending, failed or tip purchase to `deleted-account`, no longer strips a bought piece if keeping it failed, and clears the deletion's content marks only after the sold pieces are re-keyed. Round 9 (lead rulings 17 Oct 2026): one more additive migration (the index in #1), no route, field or contract change (contract:build twice equal, no diff). Behaviour: the deletion's mark of a piece is read by the piece alone (a purge or a cancel landing between a buyer's read and the check no longer answers 404); the purge moves the mark of a kept or stripped piece to `deleted-account` in the same transaction that re-keys the piece (such a row stays, it holds no personal data); only a piece that was `live` when the deletion started is kept for buyers (the mark's `priorStatus`), a piece that was removed, rejected or pending is treated like an unsold one (stripped, its files deleted) and its buyers get 404 before, during and after; the contact-detail scrub reads one number at a time in linear time. |

| MONEY-03 #7 | `src/components/Money.tsx` line height and tracking | SHARED-01. (a) The outer Text keeps `lineHeight: size` (the kit's `line-height:1`). On the web only (`Platform.OS === 'web'`) the ₦ and kobo runs get `lineHeight: size * 0.72`: in CSS each run inheriting the figure's absolute line height pushed the line box past `size` (44 at 40 from the kobo, as MONEY-03 measured; 41 from the platform-font ₦). Measured after on the web export: 40 px at size 40, light and dark. On iOS and Android the runs carry no line height of their own, because one paragraph has one line height there (iOS takes the first run's paragraph style, Android the last line-height span), so a run's 72% would set the whole line and clip it. Jest asserts the style trees for web, iOS and Android; the phone itself is V10, pending the first Android build. (b) Tracking is the kit's -0.025em as the named constant `amountTracking` citing kit.js line 13 (R-22); the design token `--tracking-money` (-0.03em) stays in `design/_ds` and `Money` no longer reads it. | as above |
| MONEY-03 #8 | `src/components/Avatar.tsx` (one component with UI-02's) and new `src/components/CurrencyFlag.tsx` | SHARED-01. Cause: an ID-number secret (`A` + 8 digits) can equal a run inside a stand-in id (`01ENT`/`01PER`/`01ACC` + 21 uppercase hex); about 2.8 in 10,000 per run. Change: when the scan finds a hit it now names `Table.column (secret shaped A99999999)` (the shape, never the secret) by a helper that runs only on a hit; the assertion `expect(hits).toEqual([])` and the SQL that finds a hit are unchanged. Round 2 (lead ruling, 17 Oct): the generator now draws ID-number secrets as `Q` + 8 digits (`person()`, one line), because `Q` is not a hex digit and starts no id prefix, so no stand-in id can hold it; `ulid()`, uuids, BVN and NIN are untouched (BVN and NIN stay at about 2.5 in a million per run). | done in FIX-58 |
